### Page 3

3
Cathay’s View市場策略思維
市場焦點
1. 市場焦點:
• 重要企業公布財報, 牽動AI類股走勢: Anthropic發布IPO公開說明書, CSP與記憶體大廠陸續公布財報,
個股走勢分化。
• 央行按下暫停鍵, 但不代表升息週期結束: 美、日、歐央行均選擇按兵不動, 但12月升息機率仍偏高。
• 殖利率快速回落, 創2023以來單日最大跌幅: AI融資疑慮再次發酵, 實質利率回落使債券久違重拾避險
資產角色。
2. 後續看點:
• 11/2美國製造業PMI、11/3-11/4美國期中選舉、11/4美國服務業PMI、11/6美國非農與失業率
策略思維
1. 股市−選舉前波動易放大, 但長多趨勢尚未結束: 就歷史數據而言, 美國期中選舉前股市表現往往較差, 但AI
需求仍未見終點, 強勁基本面支撐下股市仍有高點可期。
2. 債市−殖利率飆升暫緩, 但高盤格局不易改變: 近期債券暫時重拾避險資產角色使殖利率明顯回落, 但AI相關
企業發債量攀升的趨勢仍在, 且通膨快速回落不易, 預期殖利率仍偏向高檔震盪。

3. 匯市−美元指數漲勢暫緩, 但強美元仍將維持一段時間: 雖近期美元指數漲勢趨緩, 但Warsh決心抗通膨並重
建聯準會獨立性, 加上歐洲動盪未平, 美元短期內轉弱難度偏高。

Cathay’s View
Anthropic營收成長驚人, 預估Q3營收超過上半年總和 Anthropic未來算力採購計畫
資料來源: Anthropic預估, 預估期間為2026Q3, 截至2026/10/26。 資料來源: Anthropic, 截至2026/10/26。
• Anthropic IPO公開說明書指出, 今年Q2營收116億美元, 年增近14倍, 季增144%, 並預估Q3營收168億美元, 年增
11倍, 季增45%, 超過今年上半年營收總和, 營收成長驚人。此外, Q3營收是同期算力支出的兩倍, 預估Q4營收是
算力支出的1.7倍, 展現公司強勁的AI營收變現能力。
• Anthropic亦定調公司核心策略, 未來將擴大投入, 透過提升模型、降低token成本, 帶動客戶使用量增加, 進而帶動
營收成長, 並再度擴大投資。公司預估至2036年, 將投入約8000億美元採購算力, 為近一年投資的50倍, 反映AI需
求展望強勁。
科技｜Anthropic營收年增逾10倍, 未來將再採購50倍算力, AI需求展望強勁

Cathay’s View
主要雲端服務商財報狀況
註: 基於公平比較, EPS指GAAP EPS數值。
資料來源: Bloomberg, 國泰世華銀行整理, 截至2026Q3財報。
• 隨著大型 CSP 業者陸續公布財報 , 除了企業獲利表現 , 自由現金流是否穩定或改善 , 也成為市場關注的焦點 。
SpaceX由虧轉盈, 自由現金流也大幅改善, 帶動股價於盤後大漲7.8%。微軟的自由現金流雖小幅下滑, 但仍穩定
維持在百億美元水準, 盤後股價同樣上漲。反觀Meta、谷歌與亞馬遜, 則因自由現金流惡化, 導致盤後股價重挫。
• 不過, 無論各家業者的股價表現如何分歧, 其共同點都是肯定AI需求依然強勁, 且已實質反映在營收成長上, 同時持
續擴大資本支出。其中, SpaceX雲端收入成長8倍, 谷歌雲端收入成長102%, 微軟Azure成長48%,亞馬遜AWS年增
39%, 均展現強勁的成長動能。
日期 公司 EPS*26Q2 EPS*26Q3 自由現金流26Q2(億) 自由現金流26Q3(億) 盤後表現(%) 重要內容

10/21 SpaceX -0.09 0.35 -159 -99 +7.8 Q3營收年增161%, 且從前季虧損轉為獲利 44億美元。人工智慧營收從去年8.1億美元跳升至75.6億美元。
10/22 Meta 6.18 3.83 17.5 -11.5 -6.7 營收成長26%, 優於預期, 但法律和解費用, 獲利大幅下滑。AI改善核心業務, 小幅調升今年資本支出至1450億美元。
10/29 谷歌 9.11 2.12 -58.6 -90.1 -10.0 谷歌Q3營收成長25%至1253億美元, 其中, clouds成長102%, 但服務收入下滑至10%, 此外,  現金流缺口擴大。
10/29 微軟 4.81 4.92 196 186 +2.8 營收達923億美元, 年增19%, 其中, Azure營收成長從44%加速至48%, 均優於預期, 此外, 自由現金流依舊穩定。
10/30 亞馬遜 5.75 1.89 -76 -424 -6.0 營收年增12%至2019億美元, 其中, AWS年增率加速至39%, 但資本支出擴大導致自由現金流缺口擴大。

股市｜自由現金流好壞, 主導近期CSP的股價走勢, 但均擴大AI投資力度

Cathay’s View
台股指數創新高, 但獲利成長更快, 估值反而下降 台積電資本支出快速上升
資料來源: Bloomberg預估, 截至2026/10/30。 資料來源: 台積電, JP預估, 截至2026/10/30。
• 台股指數雖拉回, 但企業獲利持續走高, 使台股本益比進一步降至近期低檔, 顯示AI投資造成資金吃緊、殖利率走
揚的現象, 對評價壓力加大。這也使得高本益比的族群拉回時壓力更大, 企業獲利成長性較佳的類股具下檔支撐。
• 台積電對於AI長期成長看法未變, 在持續擴大資本支出時也深入評估客戶端風險 , 由於未來3年台積電資本投資仍
將遠高於過去3年, 預料明年缺貨現象仍將普遍出現, 考量AI需求與企業獲利趨勢延續, 台股拉回仍可留意布局機會。
台股｜AI投資疑慮使台股評價承壓, 但企業獲利成長趨勢延續, 可留意布局機會

Cathay’s View
SK hynix獲利持續創新高, 營利率維持高檔 SK hynix財報會議重點
韓股｜匯率逆風下財報仍超預期, SK hynix重申樂觀展望
• SK hynix公布26Q3財報, 營業利潤83.3兆韓元、營業利潤率79.6%均創歷史新高, 均優於市場預期。儘管淨利因匯
兌與業外投資損失而不及預期, 但核心業務表現強勁, 股價大漲10.9%。
• 管理層正面釐清多項原先市場擔憂的議題, 並重申AI需求仍然極為強勁, 明後兩年供不應求的狀況將持續。雖從獲
利年增率的角度而言, 2027年很難優於2026年, 但回顧過去記憶體類股的歷史週期, 只要獲利能夠持續增長而不衰
退, 股價仍然存在上行空間。
資料來源: Bloomberg預估, 截至2026/10/28。 資料來源: SK hynix財報會議, 截至2026/10/28 。

記者會重點一覽10月維持利率不變, 2Y仍反應升息預期
資料來源: Bloomberg, 2026/10/29。 資料來源: Fed, 國泰世華銀行整理。
10月FOMC｜觀望9月緊縮效果, 重申捍衛通膨決心
10月FOMC (10/28)
經濟
• 經濟強勁擴張: 預估第三季成長3%以上, 資本支出佔
GDP比重處於歷史高位, 由科技領域逐步擴張。經濟
擴張源於供給側生產力, 而非總體需求過熱。
通膨
• 捍衛2%PCE目標: 通膨已高於目標近 6年, Warsh強
調Fed負有絕對責任, 無軟性通膨目標。
• 油價推升短期通膨預期, 中長期Swaps依然錨定。
升息
傳導效果
• 9月升息後, 金融與信用情勢已如預期緊縮。
• 高利率穿透力 : 高科技產業的信用利差已出現擴大 ,
貨幣緊縮對前沿科技資本形成融資約束力。
政策展望
• 通膨控管並非綁定單一數據, 動態評估最佳政策立場。
• 會議調整: 未來維持每年 8次FOMC會議, 惟2027年
調整一次為「戰略研討」, 2028年新增至兩次。

Cathay’s View
10年債利率跌回5%下方, 惟期限溢價仍高 TIPS回落, Breakeven相對平穩
資料來源: Bloomberg, ACM為紐約聯準會之期限溢價預估模型, 截至2026/10/27。
註:名目利率 ≈ 實質利率(TIPS) + 通膨預期(Breakeven Rate)。
資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026/10/27。
• 市場對AI投資回收進度與獲利轉換能力產生疑慮, 帶動全球科技股修正; 資金流向避險資產, 帶動美國10年期公債
殖利率於10/22單日下跌16.6bps, 目前於5%附近震盪。
• 從利率結構觀察, 10年Breakeven Rate大致維持在2.3%: 呼應主席Warsh所言, 油價拉高民眾「短期通膨預期」,
但中長期通膨指標依然錨定。相較之下, 10年TIPS持續攀升為Fed升息帶動實質利率走升、信用情勢收緊。
• 此外, 美國財政紀律、美國公債供給增加、AI Capex帶動企業發債需求, 仍將支撐期限溢價處於高檔; 雖短線長端

利率受買盤影響回落, 但結構性因素驅使長端利率中樞位居高位的情勢未改。
債市｜避險資金回流, 推動美債利率回落; 惟結構性因素使期限溢價仍高

BOJ上調2027與2028財年核心通膨預期 升息預期較上次會議後變動不大
資料來源: BOJ預估,截至2026/10/30。 資料來源: Bloomberg預估,截至2026/10/30。
• 會議重點: 1)政策利率維持於1.25%不變。2)委員會以8:1投票通過, 委員高田創支持升息一碼。3)BOJ上調2027與
2028財年核心通膨預期。
• 未來展望: 日本央行重申目前通膨已接近2%目標, 並稱經濟已溫和復甦, 日本已進入新階段, 需關注通膨上行風險。
市場亦預期12月仍有升息一碼空間, 升息預期較上次會議變動不大。

美元高檔徘徊, 新台幣壓力未散 10月外資賣壓沉重, 為新台幣疲弱主因
資料來源: Bloomberg,截至2026/10/29。 資料來源: Bloomberg,截至2026/10/29。
• 10月FOMC會議雖按兵不動, 但油價居高不下, 市場仍預期未來聯準會有升息空間, 美元指數維持高檔震盪, 延續亞
幣壓力。
• 近期美台股市反覆震盪, 帶動外資在台股的獲利了結賣壓, 成為新台幣近期走弱主因。央行雖有進場干預, 但均以
維持當日匯率穩定為主, 未干預新台幣短期方向。後續若股市打底, 外資賣壓有望減輕。

### Page 15

Cathay’s View
會計項目導致帳面巨額虧損, 但實際已出現淨獲利 Q2經調整營利率翻正, 反映獲利能力強勁
註: 公司估值上升, 使融資工具價值增加, 因此需認列帳面損失, 但不造成實質現金流出。
資料來源: Anthropic, 截至2026/6/30。
註: Non-GAAP營業利益率 = (GAAP營業利益-金融負債公允價值損益調整)/營收。
資料來源: Anthropic, 截至2026/6/30。
• Anthropic Q2 GAAP淨利為-663億美元, 但主因是隨估值增加, 公司融資工具帳面價值同步增加, 形成帳面損失, 但
不造成實際支出。排除會計評價損益調整後, 2026上半年non-GAAP淨利達27億美元, 獲利已轉正。
• 此外, Q2 non-GAAP營利率達19%, 預估Q3亦能維持正向, 反映公司獲利能力已有一定基礎, 未來可望持續加速。
未來投資人可關注Anthropic營收增速是否保持高速成長, 以及non-GAAP營利率與淨利是否持續改善, 作為公司

AI投資變現能力與算力需求前景觀察指標。

• 微軟最新公布2027財年第一季財報, 營收達923億美元, 年增19%, 調整後每股盈餘4.89美元, 均優於市場預期。其

Google具備自研TPU、Gemini模型等完整AI生態系, 有利於降低對外部晶片的依賴, 長線展望仍佳。

通膨上行風險仍存 升息預期較9月會議有所降溫, 市場預估未來2~3碼空間
資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026/9。
註: 比較與9月會議之升息預期差異。
資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026/10/29。
• 歐洲央行(ECB)如預期維持三大利率不變 (DF/MRO/MLF分別維持在2.50%/2.65%/2.90%)。隨能源價格上漲推升
通膨上行風險與經濟下行壓力 ; 目前政策關鍵在於管控通膨風險 , 同時評估能源衝擊對實體經濟的影響性 。此外,
總裁Lagarde宣布將於明年2月提前卸任, 增添繼任角力與政策不確定性。
• 儘管面臨領導層異動, 仍預計ECB維持偏鷹政策立場: 10月聲明中強調基本通膨(Underlying inflation)大致維持穩定,
且薪資成長未顯著走升; 但若能源衝擊規模持續擴大, 仍需觀望是否產生二輪通膨效應。
• 隨美伊局勢持續膠著, 全球原油運輸與供應鏈不確定性高企。在通膨風險未完全消退下, 預計ECB將維持數據導向

與漸進升息的彈性空間; 目前期貨市場仍定價未來一年具2~3碼的升息空間, 相較9月會議略有降溫。

國家隊進出狀況 A股指數與評價走勢
中國｜外需撐起GDP, 政治邏輯重塑定價, 大型政策刺激暫且無望, 指數需時間築底
• Q3 GDP年增4.5%, 較上季4.3%回升, 前三季累計達4.6%, 落全年4.5~5%目標區間, 「穩中有升」反而讓政策刺激
期待落空。GDP結構並不均衡, 出口與高端製造良好; 但民間消費僅溫和擴張、投資持續下滑, 房地產依舊拖累。
• 另方面, 人民日報先以「全面從嚴治黨」為五中全會定調。將長征、遵義會議、毛澤東核心確立, 串聯至「兩個確
立」與「十五五」規劃。黨敘事把組織置於發展之上, 呼應「管黨治黨越有效, 經濟發展的保障越有力」的主張。
• 考量數據與治理取向, 1)短線行情偏淡、指數需時間築底, 因為大規模刺激期待落空, 加上國際氣氛讓AI硬體股跟

跌, 資金只能轉向金融股暫待; 2)中長線在黨建先於發展的邏輯下, 資金還是只能流向國企色彩高、與政策對齊如
半導體、能源安全、軍工等類股。

魯拉的政策方向對於財政負擔明顯較重 市場提前定價魯拉下台, 選情逆轉後股匯雙殺
巴西｜現任總統魯拉意外逆轉勝, 財政可持續性疑慮使巴西面臨股匯雙殺
• 巴西現任總統魯拉於第二輪投票中逆轉勝, 出乎市場預期。由於魯拉的政策將使巴西面臨較大的財政風險, 且原先
市場早已定價小波索納洛(其父2022敗選後被控政變罪, 遭判處監禁27年)將勝出, 故結果出爐後, 巴西的股匯市大
幅下跌。
• 根據最新消息, 小波索納洛拒絕承認選舉結果, 同時無視限制出境令前往美國尋求庇護。考量原先魯拉與川普關係
不睦, 不排除近期美國再對巴西祭出制裁措施, 美巴雙邊風險上升或將使近期巴西金融市場持續動盪。
資料來源: Bloomberg, 國泰世華銀行整理, 截至2026/10/28。 資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026/10/28。

兆豐國際商業銀行 徵信處 
中華民國一一五年十一月二日

國際經濟金融週報

### Page 2

本報告係根據研究資料及各項可靠數據作成，兆豐銀行保留報告內容所有權利，未經本行許可，請勿引
用相關內容。

1

國際經濟金融週報內容摘要 
美 
國 
美國經濟維持穩健擴張，通膨壓力略升溫，Fed 維持利率不變，期中選舉進入
最後衝刺階段 
經濟 
⚫ 美國第三季實質 GDP 季增年率(初值)由上季的 2.2%升至 3.4%，成長主
要受民間消費 、 企業投資及庫存回補帶動 。 其中個人消費貢獻2.52 個百
分點、私人投資貢獻 3.27 個百分點，惟淨出口拖累 3.32 個百分點，整
體經濟仍維持穩健擴張。 
⚫ 9 月美國新屋銷售年化 67.6 萬戶，月減 0.6%、年減 5.3%，主要是中西
部及南部地區銷售下滑拖累整體表現。房價雖較上月回升，但仍低於去
年同期；供給月數 8.4 個月，顯示市場仍呈供給充裕、需求偏弱格局。 
⚫ 美國 9 月個人消費支出(PCE)物價指數年增率由 3.4%升至 3.6%，核心
PCE 物價指數年增率亦由 3.0%升至 3.1%，通膨再度升溫。儘管實質所
得受物價上漲影響而下滑，消費支出仍持續成長，反映家庭需求及內需
動能仍具韌性。 
⚫ 10 月 FOMC 會議以 10 比 2 決議將聯邦基金利率目標區間維持於 3.75%
至 4.0%。決策聲明指出，美國經濟活動表現強勁，就業市場變化不大，
通膨持續高於 2%目標，將依後續數據評估適當政策立場，以確保通膨
回歸目標。兩名委員主張升息 1 碼，顯示內部對通膨風險仍存分歧。主
席 Kevin Warsh 強調將防止能源價格上漲擴散至整體物價 ， 並宣布2027
年起部分 FOMC 會議改為策略討論會議，以強化長期政策研議。 
重要 
事件 
⚫ 10 月 26 日路透社民調顯示，川普施政支持率 32%，略高於前週；國會
選舉政黨支持度則由民主黨 47%領先共和黨 36%。截至 10 月 31 日，
提前及郵寄投票已逾 5,100 萬人。整體而言，期中選舉結果將影響未來
國會版圖、施政推動及貿易政策方向，備受市場關注。 
短評 
⚫ 美國第三季 GDP 成長優於前季，主要受民間消費、企業投資及庫存回
補帶動，顯示內需與企業活動仍具韌性；惟新屋銷售持續疲弱、房市需
求未明顯改善，反映高利率環境仍抑制部分經濟活動。另一方面，PCE
通膨再度升溫，核心通膨降幅有限，顯示物價壓力仍存。在經濟穩健且
通膨高於目標情況下，Fed 維持利率不變並釋出偏鷹訊號，後續貨幣政
策料將維持審慎，持續關注通膨與就業數據變化。

一、 美國—經濟維持穩健擴張，通膨壓力略升溫，Fed 維持利率不變，期中選
舉進入最後衝刺階段 
2026 年第三季美國實質國內生產毛額(GDP)季增年率初值為 3.4%， 高於第二
季之 2.2%，整體景氣持續穩健擴張。就成長貢獻而言，個人消費支出仍為經濟成
長主要支柱，貢獻 2.52 個百分點，其中服務消費及商品消費分別貢獻 1.89 個百
分點及 0.63 個百分點 ， 反映民間需求維持穩健 ； 私人投資則貢獻3.27 個百分點 ，
為本季成長最大亮點，其中固定投資貢獻1.66 個百分點，而企業庫存回補亦額外
貢獻 1.61 個百分點。對外貿易方面，淨出口拖累經濟成長 3.32 個百分點，其中
出口減少造成 0.68 個百分點負面影響；進口增加則拖累 2.64 個百分點。政府消
費支出與投資則受聯邦政府非國防支出增加帶動，貢獻 0.90 個百分點。此外，第
三季個人消費支出(PCE)物價指數及核心 PCE 物價指數分別年增 2.1%及 2.5%，
均低於第二季水準。整體而言，美國第三季經濟成長主要受民間消費、企業投資
及庫存回補支撐 ， 雖貿易逆差擴大對GDP 形成明顯拖累 ， 但內需表現仍具韌性 ，
顯示美國經濟基本面維持穩健。 
2026 年 9 月美國新屋銷售經季節性調整後年化戶數為 67.6 萬戶，較上月減
少 0.6%， 並較去年同期下降5.3%， 顯示在高利率環境下住宅需求仍較去年疲弱 。
地區別表現分歧，其中東北部及西部分別較上月增加 28.0%及 21.4%，惟中西部
及南部分別減少 7.3%及 6.6%，尤其占全美銷售比重最大的南部地區回落，成為
拖累整體銷售的主因。價格方面，9 月新屋銷售中位價為 40.04 萬美元，較上月
上升 3.9%，但較去年同期下跌 3.9%，儘管房價高於上月，惟仍面臨修正壓力。
供給方面，9 月底待售新屋數量為 47.6 萬戶，較上月減少1.0%，較去年同期減少
2.3%；按目前銷售速度計算，供給月數為 8.4 個月，低於上月的 8.5 個月，但高
於去年同期的 8.2 個月。整體而言，9 月美國新屋銷售較前月小幅下滑且年減幅
度擴大，反映購屋需求仍未明顯改善；雖然部分地區銷售回升且庫存略有下降，
但供給月數仍維持相對偏高水準，顯示新屋市場仍處於供給較充裕、需求偏弱的
格局，後續仍須觀察利率走勢及就業市場變化對住宅需求的影響。 
2026 年 9 月美國個人消費支出(PCE)物價指數月增 0.5%， 高於8 月之 0.3%，

年增率則由 3.4%升至 3.6%； 扣除食品及能源後之核心PCE 月增率維持於 0.3%，
年增率由 3.0%微升至 3.1%，能源價格回升及服務價格上漲持續對整體物價形成

0.2%，個人消費支出則月增 0.5%，增幅高於所得成長，顯示家庭消費需求仍具韌
性；惟受物價上漲影響，實質可支配所得月減0.3%，實質個人消費支出則持平，
反映名目支出增加部分已被物價上升所抵銷。整體而言，美國 9 月消費活動維持
穩健，內需仍為經濟成長提供支撐，但通膨壓力較前月略有升溫，物價回落至聯
準會 2%目標之過程仍具挑戰，後續仍須持續關注能源價格變化、核心通膨走勢
及消費動能對聯準會貨幣政策方向之影響。 
聯準會(Fed)於 2026 年 10 月 FOMC 會議以 10 比 2 表決結果，決定將聯邦基
金利率目標區間維持於 3.75%至 4.00%不變，符合市場預期；謹將本次會議聲明
及主席 Kevin Warsh 記者會重點摘錄如下：(一)聯準會認為美國經濟活動持續以
強勁步調擴張，較前次聲明所稱之「穩健擴張」進一步上修對經濟表現之評價；
儘管地緣政治情勢使經濟前景不確定性仍偏高，但國內支出維持韌性，生產力成
長與資本投資表現強勁，就業增幅大致與勞動力成長同步，失業率變動不大，顯
示整體經濟基本面仍具相當韌性；(二)通膨仍高於聯準會 2%目標，委員會表示需
累積更多經濟與物價資訊，以評估適當政策立場，確保通膨適時回歸 2%目標，
並重申將致力維持物價穩定 ；(三)本次決議出現兩張反對票 ， 聯準會理事Lisa Cook
及克里夫蘭聯邦準備銀行總裁 Beth Hammack 主張升息 1 碼，反映部分委員對通
膨風險仍抱持較為謹慎態度；(四)主席 Kevin Warsh 於記者會表示，近期聯邦公
開市場委員會討論焦點多集中於物價穩定，特別是在近期能源及相關價格再度上
揚情況下，聯準會有責任避免個別產業價格上漲擴散至整體經濟，並確保通膨預
期維持穩定；(五)Warsh 並宣布調整 FOMC 會議安排，2027 年仍將維持每年 8 次
會議，但其中 1 次將改為專門討論貨幣政策策略之會議，不進行利率表決或發布
政策聲明，並預期 2028 年將有 2 次會議採取此模式，以騰出更多時間進行長期
政策與重大議題研議。整體而言，本次會議雖維持利率不變，但聯準會仍認為通
膨問題尚未完全解決，後續政策方向仍將取決於通膨、勞動市場及整體經濟數據
表現。 
根據 10 月 26 日路透社最新民調，總統川普施政支持率為 32%，較前一週之
31%略為回升；在國會選舉政黨支持度方面， 47%的登記選民表示傾向支持民主

二、歐元區—歐洲央行如期暫停升息，12 月升息機率較高 
 歐元區第三季 GDP 季增率為 0.4%，但有部份是受到愛爾蘭經濟成長率大幅
波動的扭曲，若剔除愛爾蘭，則歐元區第三季 GDP 季增率僅約 0.2%。該季受到
物價壓力上升 、 地緣政治緊張局勢等因素影響 ， 對消費及投資需求構成負面因素 ，
但世足賽以及德國財政支出擴張的支持下，仍使歐元區經濟保持正向表現。就國
別而言，德、法、義、西GDP 季增率分別為 1.2%、-0.1%、0.8%、2.7%；其中德
國經濟活動受到財政擴張的提振；法國的消費支出雖強勁，但受到投資減緩和進
口增加拖累；義大利製造業產出萎縮拖累成長表現；西班牙在住宅營建熱潮的支
持下， 經濟活動持續優於其他國家， 但歐盟NGEU 計畫到期以及政府財政整頓力
道，西班牙後續經濟動能可能會逐漸放緩。 
 歐洲央行在 10 月 29 日宣布維持政策利率不變，主要是近期歐元區各國殖利
率普遍上升 ， 對經濟造成限制性因素 ， 且不確定性可能會隨著時間的推移而累積 ，
從而拖累經濟成長。不過，歐洲央行仍認為近期高漲的能源價格對通膨的壓力相
當大，使央行考慮進一步採取貨幣政策措施因應。因此，除非經濟成長受到超出
預期的衝擊、能源價格下跌或金融環境急劇收緊，一般預期歐洲央行在 12 月的
政策會議將會宣布升息一碼。 
三、日本—日本央行暫停升息，通膨壓力續存，升息預期升溫 
 9 月工業生產月增 2.2%，扭轉 8 月受天災造成生產中斷而下滑的情勢，從細
項來看，汽車、電子產品及生產機械產量均呈回升，其中生產機械已連續第四個
月成長，反映 AI 相關資本支出需求持續暢旺；庫存月增 2.5%，石化產品庫存亦
逐步回補。展望未來，製造商預估 10 月及 11 月產量將分別月增 5.1%及 0.8%，

顯示在 AI 及資本支出需求、汽車復產與庫存回補支撐下，短期工業生產動能可
望維持強勁。 
 9 月零售月減 0.7%，主要受雨季抑制家庭外出消費，以及先前受新規上路前
換機需求及汽車稅減免政策帶動的耐久財消費動能逐漸減弱，致整體消費動能偏
弱；惟服裝及一般商品銷售受假期延長與節慶需求支撐而有所回升。另 10 月消
費者信心指數下降 0.4 點至 35.0，連續第二個月下滑，反映消費者對物價上漲及
實質購買力下降的憂慮加深，預期未來一年通膨率超過 2%的消費者比重亦升至
79.8%。 展望未來， 隨天候等暫時性不利因素逐漸消退 ，加上所得成長仍具韌性，
第四季消費動能可望回升；惟通膨持續升溫，加上汽油補貼逐步退場，恐進一步
侵蝕購買力，為消費復甦的下行風險。 
 10 月東京核心 CPI 年增 2.6%，石油相關成本上漲已由核心商品逐步擴散至服
務價格，食品及生鮮食品價格亦偏強。雖目前能源、汽油補貼等政府措施仍抑制
通膨 ， 但隨12 月相關補貼逐步退場 ， 成本壓力將進一步反映於商品及服務價格 ，
預期至 2026 年底潛在通膨仍可能高於央行的 2%目標，通膨黏性值得持續關注。 
 日本央行於 10 月 29、30 日召開貨幣政策會議 ， 維持政策利率於1.25%不變，
主要係仍需觀察先前升息的累積效果 ， 但整體政策訊號仍偏鷹派 。 本次決議以8：
1 通過，唯一反對的政策委員高田創主張再升息 1 碼至 1.5%，反映央行內部對通
膨上行風險仍具警戒 。 日本央行指出通膨已大致達到與 2%通膨目標相符的水準 ，
同時將 2027 財年的核心 CPI(不含生鮮食品)預測由 2.4%上調至 2.6%，顯示目前
通膨情勢仍支持貨幣政策進一步正常化 。央行總裁表示 ， 目前金融環境仍屬寬鬆 ，
未來若經濟及物價走勢符合預期，仍將適時調升政策利率，市場因而持續關注後
續會議再度升息的可能性。此外，日本央行亦決定自 2027 年 1 月起，將 ETF 出
售規模由約 3,300 億日圓提高至 8,600 億日圓，加速資產負債表正常化進程。 
四、中國—工業部門成本轉嫁能力有限，使利潤率持續萎縮 
1-9 月工業部門營業收入、利潤年增率分別為 15.5、13.1%，其中營業收入年
增率較上期(1-8 月)大增 8.9 個百分點，但利潤年增率下降 4.5 個百分點。受惠於
金屬、塑化原物料上漲，加上 AI 需求擴張，以及政府推動高科技產業發展等因
素，營收成長貢獻度較高者包含有色金屬加工(營收、利潤年增 17.5%、79.7%)、
計算機、通信和其他電子設備製造業(營收、利潤年增 21%、103.8%)、化學原料

用相關內容。

及製品(營收、利潤年增 9.4%、49.2%)。此外，電氣機械及器材業、汽車業營收年
增率雖分別為 11.3%、2.6%，但獲利分別年減 5.9%、22.8%。非金屬礦物製品業
營收、獲利下滑9.7、46.9%，黑色金屬加工業營收、獲利分別年減1.5%、73.3%，
反映產業持續進行減產所致。整體而言，工業部門營收受到物價上漲帶動，但成
本轉嫁能力有限，反而降低利潤表現；部分產業受惠於短期原物料價格波動，以
及受到政策推動的高科技產業表現較佳，惟部分產業因陷入產能調整週期，或政
府減少支持等因素而表現較弱，產業分化明顯。 
五、印度及東南亞—泰、菲進出口創歷史新高，泰國央行按兵不動以支撐經濟 
(1) 新加坡：9 月消費者物價指數(CPI)年增率小幅回落至 2.2%、核心通膨率自 8
月的 2.2%降至 2%，反映交通運輸（7.4%） 、服裝鞋帽（2.1%）及家庭耐用品
和服務 （0.2%）價格漲幅顯著放緩，此外，由於行業競爭壓力，資訊通信技術
服務價格的進一步回落也抑制整體服務價格的上漲。隨著 11 月電力和天然氣
價格下調生效，預計 2026 年第四季核心通膨將進一步放緩。 
(2) 泰國：9 月出口額達 384.4 億美元、年增 24.0%，創歷史新高，主要得益於工
業產品 出口成長 25.4%，其中， 以電腦及相關設備（ 59.4%）、電話及零件
（117.5%）及電路板（45.9%）成長最為顯著，對美國出口額年增 50.1%，對
中國和日本的出口額分別成長 18.4%和 19.0%。9 月進口額 443 億美元、年增
50.4%，創歷史新高，因出口導向製造業生產需求及全球能源價格的上漲，主
要類別進口額均有所成長，其中，資本貨物(電腦、機械零件、電路板等)進口
大增 92.3%，燃料、原材料和中間產品進口增幅均逾 4 成、運輸設備在飛機與
航空設備進口大增 12 倍下成長 24%。9 月貿易逆差大幅擴大至 59 億美元，累
計 1 至 9 月 ， 貿易逆差達 437 億美元 ， 出口額成長19.6%、進口額成長 37.8%。
泰國央行 10 月會議維持基準利率於 1%不變，維持寬鬆的貨幣政策立場，以
支持經濟復甦的不平衡 。經濟預估方面 ，強於預期的出口以及與科技和人工智
慧週期相關的投資，預估 2026 年和 2027 年經濟成長率將分別成長 2.4%和
2.1%，然而，高額的家庭債務、脆弱的勞動市場和疲軟的購買力繼續抑制私人
消費，使經濟成長仍低於潛在水平。通膨方面，受能源價格上漲和聖嬰現象的
影響，預計通膨率在 2027 年初將出現短暫上升，之後隨著國內需求的疲軟而
回落，2026 年和 2027 年整體通膨率平均分別為 2.1%和 1.3%。

(3) 菲律賓:9 月出口額達 98.7 億美元、 年增35.6%，主要由電子產品出口成長 72%
所帶動，占總出口額達 70.1%，其中半導體元件、辦公設備、電子設備及零件
出口成長分別達 86%、89%、73.5%，對美國出口大增 135%(26.3 億美元、占
總出口 26.6%)，對台灣出口成長 36.8%，對越南、新加坡分別成長 488%及
30%。9 月進口額達 150.3 億美元、年增 25.7%，創歷史新高，主要係電子產
品進口成長 87.4%(半導體 144%)，礦物燃料和潤滑油 、 穀物及穀物製品進口亦
分別成長 53.3%、22.8%，韓國是主要進口來源國、占進口總額 26.1%，其次
為中國占 22.3%。進出口相抵，9 月貿易逆差擴大至 51.5 億美元，累計 1 至 9
月 ， 貿易逆差從去年同期的376 億美元擴大至 472 億美元，出口額成長 17.2%、
進口額成長 20.3%。 
六、台灣—AI 動能續強，經濟成長動能穩健 
9 月景氣對策信號續呈紅燈，綜合判斷分數為 41 分，較上月增加 1 分，燈號
續呈紅燈，其中工業及服務業加班工時，受惠 AI 相關產業生產動能維持熱絡，
由綠燈轉呈黃紅燈，其餘八項燈號均維持不變。另領先與同時指標皆呈上升，顯
示國內經濟動能維持穩健。 
主計總處 10 月 30 日公布台灣第三季經濟成長率為 14.36%，主要受 AI 需求
強勁帶動出口及企業投資大幅成長，民間消費亦維持熱絡，顯示外需動能正逐步
向內需擴散，整體經濟呈現內外皆熱。展望未來，AI 需求、半導體相關投資及企
業資本支出持續擴張，加上年底消費旺季，可望支撐短期景氣表現；惟今年經濟
高速成長墊高比較基期，明年增速料將放緩，後續仍須關注 AI 需求延續性、成
長動能向傳統產業擴散情形及金融市場波動等不確定因素。

營 運 分 析

聯發科為全球前五大 IC設計公司 
聯發科是台灣最大 IC設計公司，也是全球前五大無廠半導體(fabless)公司，
成立於 1997/05，並於 2001/07 掛牌上市。在無線通訊與數位媒體領域布局
多年。26Q3 三大產品線分為 Mobile Phone 37%、Smart Edge Platform 
58%、Power IC 5%，Mobile Phone產品以手機應用程式處理器(Application 
Processor)為主；Smart Edge Platform產品包括路由器、寬頻網路、藍芽耳
機等各種有線無線晶片、ARM架構運算單晶片、智慧電視、數位機上盒，以
及客製化晶片服務。

圖四、26Q4 AI ASIC放量 
聯發科各季營收及獲利走勢預估 (百萬元)

資料來源：聯發科；兆豐投顧預估

26Q4 ASIC上修，2027年 ASIC營收達到預估高標 
聯發科上修 26Q4資料中心 ASIC營收預估到 25億美元(前次預估 20億美元
以上)，主要因為客戶需求強勁，且爭取到供應鏈額外產能如ABF。雖然資料
中心 ASIC 的毛利率略低於公司平均，但因為供應鏈成本上升，漲價反應，
且資料中心業務的營業費用率低，營益率高於公司平均，26Q4 起加分效益
顯現。 
先前預估 2027 年可服務市場規模(SAM) 800 億美元，市佔目標 15~20%，
本次預估市佔目標達到高標。下一代 2nm ASIC專案預計 2028 年量產。 
第 2家雲端資料中心專案 design kickoff，預計 2028 年貢獻營收。公司表示
非中國客戶。 
技術佈局方面，已提前投資 Ayar Labs 佈局光通訊、CPO相關研發。448G 
SerDes已 tape out。 
26Q3 完成 39 億美元海外 CB 發行，NVIDIA、Alphabet 皆參與認購，有助
於未來 AI生態系合作。

### Page 6

Chroma ATE (2360.TW)
30 October 2026 Citi Research
6
Chroma ATE
Company description
Chroma ATE is a leading supplier of testing and instrumentation equipment,
automated testing systems (ATS), and turnkey solutions. Its products are
used for testing power electronics, semiconductor, IC, EV component,
display, and other electronics applications.
Investment strategy
We rate Chroma Buy as we expect structural and robust growth ahead, driven
by: 1) strong power tester demand for AI data center/servers; 2) expanding SLT

(system level testing) demand driven by new generation model launch and
new customers ramp-up; 3) strong metrology growth from increasing foundry
capex investment in advanced packaging; and 4) solid silicon photonics
equipment demand, with CPO a key incremental upside.
Valuation
Our target price of NT$2,900 is based on 40x 2027E P/E, at close to 1x PEG.
Chroma shares have re-rated above its historical range as its growth outlook
is now strongly tied to AI infrastructure build out. We estimate 60%+ of its

revenue are now AI-related, with strong growth across product segments
including power testers, SLT, metrology, and photonics. Chroma has a
commanding market share in most of these segments and well positioned to
capitalized on the global AI expansion.
Risks
Downside risks to achieving our target price include: 1) softer AI/CPO growth
expectation due to changes in capex outlook or production bottleneck; 2)
fewer SLT needed as a result of shortened cycle time as GPU yield rate

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.
Catalyst Watch/Short Term Views (“STV”) Ratings Disclosure:

Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is

moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds
15% against the direction of the call (unless over-ridden by the analyst). The analyst may also remove a Catalyst Watch or STV
call prior to the end of the specified period in a published research note. A Catalyst Watch/STV Upside or Downside call may be

our ratings distribution table for our Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside rating system. However, we reiterate that we do
not consider No View to be a recommendation. For all Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside calls, risk exists that the
catalyst(s) and associated share-price movement will not materialize as expected.

Grand Process Technology Corp. (3131.TWO): SoIC / PLP to support 
future GM enhancement; 3Q26 GM miss
3 November 2026 | 10:54PM HKT
GPTC reported its 3Q26 preliminary results. Revenues was pre-announced, while GM 
came in at 36.6% (down 2.3ppt QoQ; down 4.2ppt YoY), below GSe/cons. of 
39.7%/40.6%, which we attribute to an unfavorable product mix. Opex ratio further 
improved to 14.6% (vs. 17.7% in 2Q26), better than our / Bloomberg consensus 
estimates of 15.1% / 16.3%, leading OP income to +34% QoQ, or +54% YoY. Overall, 
the miss on GM leads EPS miss our / Bloomberg consensus by 9% / 14%. Despite 
3Q26 GM miss, we believe GPTC’s multi-year growth outlook remains intact, with 
continuous capacity increase to better capture the demand from CoWoS capacity 
expansion, along with product mix upgrade toward SoIC and PLP , carrying higher 
content value. Maintain Buy.

Earnings revision: We factor in 3Q26 results and cut EPS by 4% / 2% / 0% in 
2026-28E, mainly on lower GM, as we slowdown the pace of product mix upgrade 
toward SoIC and PLP . Despite the cut, we continue to expect GM sequential 
enhancement, with 4Q26 GM up to 37.7% (vs. 36.6% in 3Q26) and up to 40.2% / 
42.5% in 2027-28E, driven by rising SoIC and PLP contribution.

Ratings, coverage universe and related definitions
Buy (B), Neutral (N), Sell (S) Analysts recommend stocks as Buys or Sells for inclusion on various regional Investment Lists. Being assigned a Buy or Sell
on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not Covered), is

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.

AirTAC International  | Asia Pacific
October Sales -6% MoM/+32%
YoY
Here we focus on AirTAC's monthly sales, which we believe
could be a catalyst for its share price.
What's new:
• October sales came in at NT$3,565mn (-6% MoM/+32% YoY), mainly on 15%
fewer working days, as daily shipments were up 7% MoM.
• By segment, shipments of machine tools (+31% YoY), electronics (+30%
YoY), batteries (+27% YoY), machinery (+25% YoY) and textiles (+25% YoY)

outperformed during the month.
• QTD revenue reached 33% of our estimate at NT$10,918mn (-5% QoQ/+21%
YoY) and 32% of consensus at NT$11,228mn (-2% QoQ/+24% YoY).
Outlook:
• We think 4Q26 revenue is tracking in line with management guidance of
roughly flat QoQ – better than the typical seasonal decline.
• Management remains positive on the growth outlook, driven by share gains
alongside cyclical upturn tailwinds and government policy support.
• We remain OW, as the current valuation of 15x 2027e P/E is undemanding vs.

its mid-cycle average of 25x.

Valuation Methodology and Risks
AirTAC International (1590.TW)
Base case, 29x 2027e P/E. We believe this methodology better captures earnings growth
momentum for industrial automation component plays (market sentiment moves along
with business cycles). As the industry enters an up-cycle, we think applying a near-peak cycle
valuation of 29x P/E is reasonable given the stronger business outlook.
Risks to Upside
n Stronger macroeconomic climate in China

n Stronger-than-expected demand from industrial automation
Risks to Downside
n Sharper economic downturn in China
n Miniature linear guideway business development taking more time
n Larger-than-expected investment in new products but weaker-than-expected demand

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

3Q26 전사 예상 영업이익 100조원 
DS부문 예상 영업이익은 100.0조원(OPM 67.4%, YoY +1538.5%, QoQ +14.5%) 으로, 
달러환율 1,380원기준, 메모리와 비메모리 사업부문 예상 영업손익은 각각 102.0조원,    
-2.0조원으로 추정한다. 1) 메모리반도체 가격 상승세가 이어지는 가운데, HBM4 판매 
본격화에 따른 영향을 고려하면 DRAM/NAND 분기 평균 가격 상승폭은 각각 +18.0%, 
+16.0%으로 추정하고, 2)비메모리반도체 사업 적자는 지속될 전망이기 때문이다. 다만 
특별 성과급 지급에 따른 충당금 영향을 제외하고 선단공정 중심의 파운드리 가동률을 
고려하면 적자폭은 유의미하게 감소한 것으로 파악된다는 점이 긍정적이다.   
SDC부문 예상 영업이익은 1.1조원(OPM 13.0%, YoY -9.6%, QoQ +54.9%)으로, 1)국내 
주력 고객사향 폴더블 스마트폰 판매 호조가 사업부의 외형 성장에 긍정적으로 작용했을 
것으로 추정됨에도 불구하고, 2) 8.6세대 IT OLED 패널 양산 개시에 따른 감가상각비(약 
0.3조원 수준)와 북미 고객 중심의 판가 인하 압박 영향으로 전년동기대비 수익성은 약 
2%pt 훼손이 불가피할 전망이다. DX부문 예상 영업손익은 -1.1조원(YoY 적자전환, QoQ 
적자지속)으로, 메모리반도체를 포함한 IT 부품 가격 부담이 전분기대비 확대되고 있기 
때문이다. MX와 VD/DA사업부 예상 손익은 각각 -0.9조원, -0.2조원으로 추정한다.

투자의견 BUY, 목표주가 63만원으로 상향 조정  
메모리반도체의 가격 상승폭이 다소 둔화되겠지만, 2027~2028년에도 타이트한 수급 
환경이 이어질 전망이라는 점을 주목한다. 1)시장은 신규 증설과 HBM 관련 노이즈로 인한 
2028년 피크아웃 가능성을 우려하고 있으나, HBM 의 범용 DRAM 생산능력 잠식과 AI 
추론 확산에 따른 고용량 메모리 수요를 감안하면 공급 제약의 해소 시점은 예상보다 
늦어질 가능성이 높기 때문이다. 뿐만 아니라, 2)메모리 업사이클이 장기화될 경우 동사 
FCF는 과거대비 구조적으로 확대되어 보다 친화적인 주주환원 정책(2027~2029년, 
2027년 1월 예정)을 제시할 가능성도 배제할 수 없기 때문이다. 동사 2027년 연간 실적 
추정치 상향 조정에 따라 목표주가를 63만원(2027년 BPS * Target PBR 3.5배 적용)으로 
상향 조정한다. 피크아웃 우려에 따른 주가 조정은 중장기 관점에서 매수 기회다.

### Page 1

TAKING STOCK

The Reserve Bank of India (RBI) has adopted a 
multi-layered defence strategy to support the 
rupee this year.

This has entailed a combination of outright 
intervention in spot/ forwards, regulatory 
action, liquidity steps, administrative measures 
and moves to curb import -related dollar 
demand.

The rupee has appreciated by 1.8% against the 
dollar in the past five days since recent 
measures took effect, paring YTD depreciation 
to 5.5% . High oil prices, weakness in capital 
inflows, a firm dollar , tariffs/ geopolitical 
worries, energy , and risk -off sentiments have 
kept the INR under pressure since last year.

• The RBI has adopted a multi -
layered defence strategy to 
support the rupee this year. 
• Parallels with 2013 exist, 
although the circumstances 
differ. 
• Rupee gains have been 
accompanied with distortions 
elsewhere. 
• Authorities are likely not done 
and remain ready to take 
further action, if needed. 
• Scope of further rate 
increases is high.  Not a 
panacea but a necessary step.  
• If FX depreciation pressures 
return, a  larger scale of hike 
or inter-meeting move might 
also be considered.

India: Degrees of rupee defence

16 October 2026 
Radhika Rao, Senior Economist 
radhikarao@dbs.com

### Page 2

India: Degrees of rupee defence October 16, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            2

ECHOES OF 2013 WITH FEW 
DIFFERENCES

Comparisons with 2013 are apt, though 
the backdrop differs.  As for the 
catalysts, markets reacted to the US 
Fed’s taper announcement in 2013, 
which pushed US yields higher  and led 
global investors to reduce exposure to 
vulnerable EM economies. As a result, 
capital inflows weakened, exposing 
funding mismatch es for countries, 
including India, that were running wide 
current account deficits.

In 2026, markets face the spectre of 
elevated oil prices, geopolitical 
uncertainty, global competition for 
capital, a firm dollar ( DXY appreciated 
+3.8% YTD) , and US inflation/ deficits 
which have settled at levels much higher 
than pre -pandemic years. This has 
pushed up UST yields, and the US Fed 
kicked off its rate-hike cycle in Sep26.

Against this backdrop, the RBI is 
pursuing the same objective , i.e., to 
ensure rupee stability, but the nature of 
the battle differs. The defence strategy 
has shifted from ‘crisis management’ 
mode in 2013 , due to weaker domestic 
macroeconomic balances (a wide current 
account gap, high inflation, a lower 
reserve buffer, and wide fiscal deficits) , 
to a ‘terms-of-trade shock and impact of 
a firm dollar’ in 2026.

Flashpoints are  nonetheless similar, 
with reliance on portfolio financing , 
leaving the external account vulnerable 
to shifts in global risk sentiment. 
Portfolio outflows have emerged as the 
primary transmission channel through 
which external shocks have translated 
into INR depreciation pressure , 
compounded by high energy prices and a 
firm dollar.

FX PLAYBOOK

While the response framework is similar 
between 2013 and this year, the broad 
sequence and nature of macroprudential 
measures differ. This year’s multi-
layered rupee defence toolkit has 
included:

-Direct FX defence  by way of 
intervention in the spot and forwards 
market. As of Aug26, the net forward 
dollar liabilities ballooned to $200bn, 
rising by ~$63bn vs . the month before . 
Recent sell/buy FX swaps are expected 
to lower this stock in the coming months.

-Attracting dollar inflows  by way of 
special swap facilities for FCNR deposits, 
and offshore borrowings , apart from 
liberalising taxes around debt inflows,

### Page 3

India: Degrees of rupee defence October 16, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            3

amongst others. We discussed this here, 
here, here and here.

Taking a proactive stance, a portion of 
existing FX reserves could be earmarked 
against forward liabilities (from maturing 
FCNR deposits) , helping to mitigate 
concerns that deposit maturities or debt 
repayments could trigger a sharp
increase in dollar ⁠demand down the line.

-Domestic liquidity management. Bonds, 
to some degree, are paying the price for 
the authorities' efforts to defend the 
rupee against the additional headwind of 
weak global conditions.  INR liquidity 
surged on the back of the swap windows, 
prompting the RBI to undertake open 
market operations, VRRR auctions, sell -
buy swaps, among others. While these 
have been effective in absorbing 
balances, bond yields have drifted higher. 
10Y yields are at three-year highs and are 
expected to climb further  (DBSf: 7.40 -
7.50% by year-end).

-Moderate FX demand and speculation . 
Earlier in the year, the RBI had 
introduced change s to the banks’ net 
open position framework, and thereafter 
partially rolled back a few curbs. 
Prudential measures on FX exposures 
and hedging -related measures were 
announced in Oct26, which include d the 
introduction of a daily cash reserve with 
the RBI equal to 20% of the rupee 
equivalent of the notional value for INR -
linked derivative contracts exceeding 
$2mn used to hedge current account 
transactions, referred to as the Foreign 
Exchange Risk Reserve (FERR) . 
Subsequent clarifications provided 
operational details on where the FERR 
would be maintained and that it would be 
subject to audits.

Since 12 Oct, the RBI has been providing 
dollars via designated banks to the three 
state-owned oil -marketing companies 
(Indian Oil, BPCL, and HPCL) to meet the 
their daily dollar requirements, thereby 
diverting related spot-market demand.

On hedging-related matters, the limit for 
undertaking foreign exchange derivative 
transactions without proving an 
underlying commercial exposure was 
cut to $5mn from $100mn across all ADs 
and exchange -traded currency 
derivatives. Restrictions on Rebooking , 
i.e., a uthorized dealers are prohibited 
from allowing customers to rebook 
cancelled rupee -linked derivative 
contracts (though rollovers upon 
maturity remain permitted). Lastly, 
banks must obtain written confirmation 
from users that the same underlying 
exposure has not been hedged with 
another dealer.  Data from CCI , cited by

### Page 4

India: Degrees of rupee defence October 16, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            4

Bloomberg, pointed to importers’ 
forward dollar purchases rising 44% on 
the year to a monthly average of $64bn.

-Current account management . Efforts 
to contain structural FX outflows by way 
of higher duties and taxes on gold 
imports discourage non -essential 
purchases and production incentives to 
improve export competitiveness.

IMPACT

To assess whether the playbook has 
worked, we observe:

a) Rupee has enjoyed a brief respite. 
Despite oil prices at $105/bbl at the time 
of writing and elevated global yields, 
USDINR fell from near 96.80 to wards 
95.00, owing to measures announced in 
Oct26 following the central bank’s 25bp 
hike. Beyond temporary relief, global 
catalysts are likely to be back to the fore 
and weigh on the currency (see chart);

b) Sharp inflows under the swap 
facilities pushed the foreign reserves 
stock to a record high, providing 
policymakers sufficient buffer to 
navigate future shocks from a position of 
strength. A strong interventionist 
response has since led to a softening in 
the overall stock (see chart).

c) Macroprudential measures have led to 
distortions in other parts of the market . 
For instance, strong swap -related 
inflows had inorganically pushed 
onshore INR liquidity up sharply, 
necessitating absorption measures. 
Similarly, NOP restrictions and the more 
recent tighter requirements on hedging 
activity and counterparty transactions  
had some after -effects. Forward points 
have surged this week , with financial 
institutions pointing to relative illiquidity 
in the currency forwards market  on 
volumes and activity basis, and fewer 
avenues to deal with dollar supply.

### Page 5

India: Degrees of rupee defence October 16, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            5

d) Swap-related windows are a timely 
support for the country’s balance of 
payments position. From expectations of 
a third consecutive year of BOP deficit in 
FY27, the math has now swung to a 
potential surplus, even as current 
account worries remain. With energy 
prices settling north of $100/bbl, 
keeping the monthly trade deficit near 
$30bn, our FY27 current account deficit 
forecast of 1.1% of GDP faces  upside 
risks of around 0.3-0.5% of GDP. We note 
the s harp jump in ECB outflows in S ep, 
though see that as a temporary overshot 
of leverage-FCNR activity.

WHAT LIES AHEAD

Authorities are likely not done yet and 
remain ready to take further action, as 
required. RBI Governor Malhotra 
reiterated the authorities’ serious intent 
of stabilising the currency in a recent 
interview (Moneycontrol, 13 Oct), 
emphasising that “The RBI is determined 
to do whatever is necessary to ensure 
orderly movement in the foreign 
exchange market,”. He also highlighted 
that recent steps were not tantamount to 
capital control measures that were 
aimed at ensuring that forex markets 
serve genuine hedging needs rather than 
speculative bets.  Market participants 
opine that FX volumes and interest are 
likely to be subdued in a few pockets on 
the back of the recent measures, 
consequently stabilizing the rupee.

Unlike in 2013, when policy tightening 
was a part of the initial response, the 
2026 strategy has prioritised targeted 
FX and regulatory measures.  The 
sequencing suggests a deliberate effort 
to distinguish actions aimed at 
preserving currency stability from those 
intended to achieve domestic monetary 
objectives, with rates remaining a 
potential, though not immediate, tool in 
the defence framework.

However, global developments have 
altered that dynamic . Rising US yields 
and a gradual increase in the US Fed 
Funds rate (DBS baseline: a total of 75bp 
hikes) have compressed IN -US spreads. 
The recent sell -off in INR benchmark

### Page 6

India: Degrees of rupee defence October 16, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            6

bonds has effectively widened Gsec -
UST spreads across maturities, in 
contrast to the compression witnessed in 
regional high-yielders.

The latest announcements may not 
fully quell speculation over further 
policy action.

Upcoming measures might include -

-Markets anticipate relief on the long -
term capital gains tax structure on 
equities to draw in longer-duration 
inflows. This measure will boost inflows 
if it coincides with other factors that 
make domestic equities attractive for 
thematic investing , as global risk -free 
rates moderate.

-Street expectations also include steps 
to boost inward dollar repatriation by 
exporters to boost domestic dollar 
supply.

-Wider policy rate corridor akin to 2013.

-Policy rate response. While this might 
not be a panacea, it is a likely necessary 
condition. Raising the neutral rate might 
have mixed success in attracting capital 
market inflows ; though rate-sensitive 
flows are likely to get a fillip as investors 
seek attractive rate differentials in the 
region.

An adequate real rate cushion would 
also align with the evolving domestic 
inflation realities, which are expected to 
accelerate to 6% in the Oct-Dec quarter 
and remain above 5% well into 2027.

Given fluid global developments, we 
outline three scenarios on the rate path 
ahead. Our baseline assumption  is for 
75bp hikes in this cycle  (including Oct), 
spread between the Dece mber and 
February 2027 meetings. We don’t rule 
out an intermeeting rate hike before the 
December meeting, contingent on 
rupee movements.

Under the second scenario , s hould 
depreciation pressures on the rupee 
return, the 50bp hike might be brought 
forward to December , with another in 
February, taking cumulative hikes in this 
cycle to 100bp. The t hird scenario  is a 
more aggressive 150bp worth of hikes in 
this cycle, with 50bp front -loaded in 
December, and the rest spilling into 2027. 
Factors that  will determine whether 
these scenarios materialise are the 
resumption and scale of rupee 
depreciation, oil price direction, US Fed 
Funds rate increases (DBSf: total 75bps) 
and other risk-off events.

Path ahead

Despite these measures, we see limited 
room for sustained INR appreciation on 
grounds of manufacturing/ trade 
competitiveness. A challenging flows 
portfolio flows outlook leaves the d oor 
open to further gradual rupee 
depreciation subject to global 
developments, with high sensitivity to oil 
price movements and risk sentiments . A 
strong rate response will help introduce  
favourable rate differentials and reduce 
currency volatility.

Document 384: Inside the cost-of-living squeeze Households are weathering higher prices, but housing costs remain a key strain. 2 min read

Economy 
Inside the cost-of-living squeeze 
04 November 2026

Key takeaways 
• Not every essential has become less affordable. Despite the cumulative rise in prices, households are spending a slightly smaller 
share of income on gas and groceries than they did several years ago. That suggests the affordability squeeze is less broad-
based than the inflation headlines alone might imply. 
• Housing is the major exception, and the pressure extends beyond lower-income households. Both mortgages and rent have 
increased since 2019. Some lower-income renters look particularly stretched, but around one in four higher-income mortgage 
holders also spend more than half their income on housing. 
• Wage gains and changing behavior have helped households absorb higher prices. But real wage growth has mostly softened, 
which may help explain why sentiment remains weak even as consumer spending continues to grow. In fact, declining sentiment 
likely reflects the sacrifices consumers have to make in order to keep spending.

Everyday essentials take up less income than they did four years ago 
Higher prices are still top of mind for consumers, especially when it comes to affordability. But just how stretched are 
households? Using Bank of America internal data, we look at how much of their income households are spending on everyday 
essentials like gas, groceries, mortgages and rent, and how that has changed over the past four years across income groups. We 
also explore how wage growth and shifts in spending habits have helped households cope with higher prices, and why consumers 
still feel downbeat even as they keep spending. 
For example, while consumers overall spent slightly more of their income on gas and groceries compared to last year, outlays for 
these necessities are still below what they were four years ago, according to Bank of America payment and deposit data (Exhibit 
1). Combined, gas and groceries accounted for nearly 13.5% of income in 2026, 10 basis points more than last year, but almost 
half a percentage point less than four years ago. And even with price increases over the past year, most consumers only spent 
3.2% of their income on gasoline, up from 2.9% a year ago and below the 3.4% from four years ago.  
Exhibit 1: Consumers overall spent slightly more of their income on gas and groceries compared to last year, though spending on these necessities 
is still below pre-pandemic levels 
Median gas and grocery spending as a share of median income (annually, %)

Note: 2026 data is through September.

Across income groups, each cohort follows a similar pattern, though lower-income households do allocate the highest share of

Exhibit 2: All income cohorts have experienced an increase in gas spending as a share of income, offsetting a decrease in grocery spending in 2026

Source: Bank of America internal data 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE

Housing stress isn’t just a low-income story 
Housing is an altogether different picture. Whether you’re a homeowner or a renter, housing is often the biggest expense of any 
consumer, accounting for around 30% of income. Median mortgage payments have steadily risen over the past two years and 
now account for more than 30% of a homeowner’s income, according to Bank of America internal data (Exhibit 3). 
While renters have experienced some easing, they tend to set aside more of their income to housing costs (Exhibit 4). Rents as a 
share of income peaked in 2025, with renters spending more than 32% of their income on shelter. Even so, a small improvement 
helps. Due to declines in housing payments, renters have seen a significant improvement in discretionary spending over the past 
year (read more in On the move: Renovation over relocation?). Also, some housing related costs have also eased in the past year 
(read more in Energy bills: Short-term relief, longer-term challenges and Insurance costs hit the brakes).  
Exhibit 3: Mortgage payments as a share of income have risen over 
the past two years…

Note: Includes both internal (BofA) and external (non-BofA) mortgage payments.  
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE

period

BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE

Also, when it comes to housing, higher-income families typically spend more of their wages on mortgages than lower-income 
households (Exhibit 5). The converse is true for renters, with lower-income families spending around 35% of their paychecks on 
rent, five percentage points more than higher earners (Exhibit 6).

Some financially stretched households may be particularly affected by rising housing costs 
Notably, in the first nine months of the year, roughly one in four lower-income renters spent more than half of their income on 
rent, up from about one in five in 2019 (Exhibit 7). One positive note, the share of renters who spend most of their income on 
rent does seem to have declined slightly in 2026.  
Financial pressure from housing is not limited to lower-income households. Around one in four higher-income mortgage holders 
spend more than half of their income on home payments (Exhibit 8). And while only slightly more than one in five lower-income 
mortgage holders spent half of their income on housing, this is a nearly five percentage point increase compared to pre-
pandemic levels.

Wage growth and changing habits are insulating some households from inflation 
Still, some families have not seen their bills, as a share of income, rise alongside inflation. Why is this? In our view, this is likely 
because after-tax wages have increased significantly for these people. This is especially true for some lower-income households 
that have landed steady employment since the pre-pandemic era, according to Bank of America internal data and the Consumer 
Price Index (CPI) (Exhibit 9).  
Additionally, consumers have adjusted their behavior in response to higher prices. For one, we may be driving less than before 
the pandemic. Despite some increases in vehicle miles driven per capita over the past four years, we’re still getting behind the 
wheel less on average compared to 2019 levels, according to the US Census Bureau and Bureau of Transportation (Exhibit 10). 
At the same time, some households have “traded down” to less expensive stores or items and services (read more in The rise of 
value).  
Exhibit 9: Some lower-income households have seen their wages 
grow faster than inflation since 2019 
After-tax wages, based on Bank of America deposit data (three-month 
moving average, index 2019 = 100) compared to CPI inflation, based on 
Bureau of Labor Statistics (BLS) data (monthly, index 2019 = 100)

Source: Bank of America internal data, BLS 
Note: Data not available for October 2025 due to federal government shutdown. 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE

Exhibit 10: Vehicle miles driven per capita have not returned to pre-
pandemic levels 
Total vehicle miles traveled per total resident and civilian population 
estimates (monthly, index 2017 average = 100)

Source: US Census Bureau, Bureau of Transportation   
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE

Inflation is driving households to make more sacrifices 
There is one caveat to the affordability picture: wage growth among lower-income households slowed significantly throughout 
2025, though they’ve recovered since then. Furthermore, some middle- and higher-income families have not seen their pay keep 
up with inflation, at least compared to 2019 levels.  
In fact, using the ratio of after-tax wages to inflation as a proxy for “real wages,” we can see they largely declined throughout 
2022 and then again since mid-2023, according to Bank of America internal and BLS inflation data (Exhibit 11). Additionally, the 
drop in real wages is largely mirrored by a decline in the University of Michigan’s Index of Consumer Sentiment. This suggests 
that people have been feeling worse about their finances and the broader economy as their purchasing power has declined. 
Consumers are spending more than they did last year (read more in Consumer Checkpoint: Strength in numbers), but sentiment 
likely reflects the sacrifices consumers have made in order to keep spending.  
Looking ahead, in our view, the outlook for affordability is relatively encouraging: wage growth remains firm and has picked up 
for lower-income houses. That said, there are risks. BofA Global Research notes the potential for gasoline and grocery prices to 
increase. And higher mortgage rates make it difficult for those looking to make the jump from renting to homeownership.

Methodology  
Selected Bank of America transaction data is used to inform the macroeconomic views expressed in this report and should be considered in the 
context of other economic indicators and publicly available information. In certain instances, the data may provide directional and/or predictive 
value. The data used is not comprehensive; it is based on aggregated and anonymized selections of Bank of America data and may reflect a 
degree of selection bias and limitations on the data available. 
Any payments data represents aggregated spend from US Retail, Preferred, Small Business and Wealth Management clients with a deposit 
account or credit card. Aggregated spend include total credit card, debit card, ACH, wires, bill pay, business/peer-to-peer, cash, and checks.  
Any Small Business payments data represents aggregate spend from Small Business clients with a deposit account or a Small Business credit 
card. Payroll payments data include channels such as ACH (automated clearing house), bill pay, checks and wire. Bank of America per Small 
Business client data represents activity spending from active Small Business clients with a deposit account or a Small Business credit card and 
at least one transaction in each month. Small businesses in this report include business clients within Bank of America and generally defined as 
under $5mm in annual sales revenue.  
Unless otherwise stated, data is not adjusted for seasonality, processing days or portfolio changes, and may be subject to periodic revisions.  
The differences between the total and per household card spending growth rate (if discussed) can be explained by the following reasons:  
1. Overall total card spending growth is partially boosted by the growth in the number of active cardholders in our sample. This could be due to 
an increasing customer base or inactive customers using their cards more frequently. 
2. Per household card spending growth only looks at households that complete at least five transactions with Bank of America cards in the 
month. Per household spending growth isolates impacts from a changing sample size, which could be unrelated to underlying economic 
momentum, and potential spending volatility from less active users. 
3. Overall total card spending includes small business card spending while per household card spending does not. 
4. Differences due to using processing dates (total card spending) versus transaction date (per household card spending). 
5. Other differences including household formations due to young adults moving in and out of their parent’s houses during COVID. 
Any household consumer deposit data based on Bank of America internal data is derived by anonymizing and aggregating data from Bank of 
America consumer deposit accounts in the US and analyzing that data at a highly aggregated level. Whenever median household savings and 
checking balances are quoted, the data is based on a fixed cohort of households that had a consumer deposit account (checking and/or savings 
account) for all months from January 2019 through the most current month of data shown.  
Bank of America aggregated credit/debit card spending per household includes spending from active US households only. Only consumer card 
holders making a minimum of five transactions a month are included in the dataset. Spending from corporate cards are excluded. Data 
regarding merchants who receive payments are identified and classified by the Merchant Categorization Code (MCC) defined by financial 
services companies. The data are mapped using proprietary methods from the MCCs to the North American Industry Classification System 
(NAICS), which is also used by the Census Bureau, in order to classify spending data by subsector. Spending data may also be classified by other 
proprietary methods not using MCCs.  
Grocery, as discussed in this analysis, includes spending at grocery stores, warehouse clubs and dollar/discount stores.  
Lower, middle and higher household income cuts in Bank of America credit and debit card spending per household, and consumer deposit 
account data are based on quantitative estimates of each households’ income. These quantitative estimates are bucketed according to terciles, 
with a third of households placed in each tercile periodically. The lowest tercile represents ‘lower income’, the middle tercile represents ‘middle 
income’ and the highest tercile ‘higher income’. The income thresholds between these terciles will move over time, reflecting any number of 
factors that impact income, including general wage inflation, changes in social security payments and individual households’ income. The 
income and tercile in which a household is categorised are periodically re-assessed.  
Generations, if discussed, are defined as follows: Gen Z, born after 1995; Younger Millennials: born between 1989-1995; Older Millennials: born 
between 1978-1988; Gen Xers: born between 1965-1977; Baby Boomer: 1946-1964; Traditionalists: pre-1946. 
Any reference to card spending per household on gasoline includes all purchases at gasoline stations and might include purchases of non-gas 
items. 
Additional information about the methodology used to aggregate the data is available upon request.

現。此外，G7釋放緊急原油與柴油庫存後 ，
減輕市場對能源價格與通膨的部分 擔憂，亦
帶動非必需消費產業上漲。

隨中東衝突前景未明 ，供應不穩之變數短期
難解，多空因素交錯恐使油價於高檔進行反
覆波動。

► 美國經濟仍具韌性 ，惟通膨壓力猶存 。觀察
美國9月ISM服務業指數表現 ，內需活動維持
穩健，企業投入成本壓力亦有所升溫；另一
方面，受地緣政治因素影響 ，美國柴油價格
大幅攀升 ，而柴油為卡車運輸 、農業及製造
業的重要燃料 ，若價格持續維持高檔 ，恐進
一步增添通膨壓力 。考量聯準會偏鷹立場 ，
且當前通膨降溫進程不如預期 ，年底前仍保
有升息空間。
► 美國債務總額突破40兆美元，預估2026年公
眾持有聯邦債務占 GDP的比率將達 101%，
財政可持續性風險升溫 。與此同時 ，AI基礎

建設舉債熱潮 ，疊加中東局勢動盪帶來的通
膨不確定性 ，迫使投資人重新定價長天期債
券風險 ，要求更高的風險溢酬 。隨實質殖利
率上行，美債10年期殖利率揚升並持續高懸
於5%水準之上，30年期長債殖利率亦同步站
上近20年來高點 。隨長天期債券利率續揚 ，
殖利率曲線趨陡峭 ，債券配置宜先避開長天
期部位，聚焦中短天期債券鎖利機會。

### Page 6

6
投資焦點
► 距離美國期中選舉投票僅剩不到1個月，觀察
Polymarket賭盤預估數據顯示 ，眾議院選情
較為明朗，民主黨有超過9成的機率控制眾議
院。但參議院選情則尚有變數 ，今年以來兩
黨均互有領先 ，雖然數據顯示民主黨掌控參
議院機率呈現上揚 ，然隨時序進入選前最後
衝刺期 ，參議院能否亦由民主黨掌控仍待觀
察。故就選前而言 ，由於不同政黨的政策傾
向差異 ，潛在受惠產業亦有不同 ，選舉結果
的不確定性恐令投資人不易大舉押注股市。
► 另一方面 ，油價高檔徘迴 ，導致年底前通 膨
擔憂仍存 ，加上AI企業發債需求成長 、美國
財政體質惡化等 因素共同發酵 ，推動近期 美
債殖利率逐步攀高 ，並令股市預期本益 比壓
力延續 ，進而限縮了投資人短期對美股的追
價動能，短期指數恐維持震盪輪動格局 。可
留意抗波動 、體質佳且具價值優勢之標的 ，
待期中選舉結束與3Q26財報展開後，不確定
性減退將有助資金回歸長線AI題材。
殖利率攀升令美股本益比面臨壓力參議院期中選舉選情仍具變數
期中選前擾動與殖利率走高，美股短期恐仍震盪輪動

### Page 7

7
投資焦點日股
► 9月日本銀行決議升息 1碼，明確表示潛在
CPI已接近2%，未來也將繼續調高利率 ，政
策重點開始轉向防範通膨向上偏離目標 。然
因有2位委員反對升息，加上總裁植田和男會
後談話偏向中性 ，令市場解讀此次升息未如
預期鷹派，美日利差不易快速縮窄，故9月會
議後日元反而呈現利多出盡的回貶反應。
► 觀察今年以來日股面臨短線日圓升值的狀態
並未出現顯著走弱 態勢。一方面反映企業治
理改革推動股東報酬 提升已成為 支撐股價的
重要結構性因素 。另一方面 ，升息代表日本
經濟已具備一定的健康程度 ，貨幣政策正常
化將不致對需求帶來衝擊 。因此 ，隨BoJ延
續升息方向 ，將擴大銀行淨利差 ，改善其獲
利結構，並提升金融資產收益 。儘管近期整
體日股預期獲利成長走勢略顯停滯 ，然日本
銀行股 在總 體經 濟 與貨幣 政策環 境的 支持
下，獲利成長有望逆勢攀升 ，料將成為後續
資金關注重點。
日圓升值對日股影響似乎有限，關注銀行股獲利走強BoJ升息方向延續，惟其步伐仍取決於經濟與物價

BoJ政策保持正常化路線，日本銀行股有望持續受惠

### Page 8

8
股市
 公債殖利率走高雖可能壓抑 股債市場近期 動能，然而觀察美股第三季獲利年增 率有望仍為雙位數成
長，或支撐股市表現 。市場持續關注AI資本支出是否帶來獲利成長 ，短期內評價面壓力將增添市場
動盪，可分散配置於不同國家與產業 ，降低市場波動影響 。總結上述，地緣衝突、殖利率偏高及評
價面壓力等紛擾 帶來干擾，可布局防禦 股增強抗震力 。長期而言，科技及AI半導體發展趨勢不變且
基本面佳，可逢拉回時分批布局。
 歐、日股可作為分散配置重點 ，加上全球利率走揚有利銀行股 。此外，地緣衝突頻傳 ，歐洲國防軍
工仍有投資題材。
策略風格：短期可增加防禦股配置，中長
期看好AI題材如科技及半導體，非AI部分
可關注金融及醫療保健產業
區域：日本銀行股、歐洲金融、歐洲國防
債市
 全球公債殖利率攀升 ，且最新聯準會利率點陣圖顯示 ，官員預估今年底前仍有一碼升息空間 ，立場
偏向維持較高利率水平抑制通膨 發展。殖利率偏高環境建議以中短天期債券投資為主 ，可逢利率走
升時布局進行鎖利領息 。高信評債體質較佳 ，具穩定現金流與還款能力 ，聚焦風險調整後利差具吸

引力者，包含金融、科技、通訊及公用事業等產業。
 可以非美貨幣債券如歐元或澳幣計價投資級債券，分散單一貨幣風險。
債種：中短天期高信評債鎖利，產業如金
融、科技、通訊及公用事業等
衛星配置：非美貨幣債券分散風險
匯市
 聯準會升息且態度偏鷹，美元高檔盤整。
 日本銀行9月升息，但前瞻指引偏中性，未如市場預期的鷹派，日圓震盪。
美元高檔整理，日圓震盪
商品
 中東地緣衝突短期紛擾難解，偏高的油價可能對通膨及實體經濟帶來影響。
 短期受美國雙率走高有壓 ，惟美日財政赤字壓力與去美元化使全球央行買盤依舊 ，提供黃金中長期
穩定支撐。

個別客戶因年齡、投資能力、金融商品交易經驗及風險承受度不同，適合之服務及/或金融商品亦有不同，應進一步與專屬理財業務人員進行評估。投資人應徹底瞭解相關服務及金融商品之風險 ，並審慎評估個人財力、資產狀況、
理財需求及風險承受能力，獨立判斷並自行決定是否投資及承擔有關風險。投資任何商品皆具有風險，商品以往之績效不保證未來之投資收益，本公司不負責商品之盈虧，也不保證最低之收益。
本公司保留對本文件及其內容之著作權及其他相關權利，任何人未經本公司同意，不得翻印或作其他任何用途使用(包括但不限於傳送或轉發予其他人士等)。如有違反情事，本公司除不負任何責任外，亦將對未經同意之使用與散布
等行為究責。
基金警語
投資一定有風險，基金投資有賺有賠，申購前應詳閱公開說明書
【信託產品投資須知及風險預告：（請投資人審慎評估下列各項條文內容）】
1. 基金（包括具有定期配息之基金）投資具投資風險，此一風險可能使信託本金發生全部虧損。
2. 基金委託人須自負盈虧，本公司受託投資不保本不保息，除盡善良管理人注意義務外，投資所可能產生的本金虧損、匯率損失、或基金解散、清算、移轉、合併等風險，均由委託人承擔。

3. 本公司受託投資之基金經金管會核准或同意生效，惟不表示絕無風險。基金經理公司以往之經理績效不保證基金之最低投資收益；基金經理公司除盡善良管理人之注意義務外，不負責基金之盈虧，亦不保證最低之收益，委託人
申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書。
4. 投資前應詳閱風險預告及各該基金公開說明書 ，確實作好財務規劃與風險評估 。本公司受託投資之基金已備有公開說明書 (或其中譯本)或投資人須知【前述文件併揭露有關基金應負擔之費用 (境外基金含分銷費用)】，委託人可
至本公司營業處所索取或至公開資訊觀測站、基金資訊觀測站或本公司網站下載。
5. 為保護既有投資人之權益，部份境外基金設有價格稀釋調整機制及公平市價規定，投資人申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書及投資人須知。
6. 委託人瞭解並同意受託人辦理特定金錢信託業務(即委託人委託投資基金)交易時，自交易相對人取得之報酬、費用、折讓等各項利益，得作為受託人收取之信託報酬；另委託人在持有基金期間 ，受託人仍持續收受經理費分成報
酬，委託人可至受託人(即本公司)網站 (https://www.kgi.com.tw/zh-tw/)查詢基金之通路報酬。其基金經理費率及其分成費率與其他通路報酬最新變動情形，亦可至該網站查詢。

7. 後收型基金在贖回時，基金公司將依持有期間長短收取不同比率之遞延申購手續費 ，該費用將自贖回總額中扣除；另後收型基金若有收取分銷費 ，則申購手續費雖可遞延收取，惟每年仍需支付0%~1.06%的分銷費（按基金日平
均淨資產價值計算），可能造成實際負擔費用增加（分銷費依各基金公司而有不同，請參考本公司後收型基金投資費用暨特約事項及各基金之投資人須知），另該費用已反映於每日基金淨值中，委託人無需額外支付。
8. 委託人之交易如經基金公司認定為短線交易者，基金公司或本公司可拒絕受理申請轉換或申購要求，並可請求支付一定比例之贖回費用或其他相關費用，費用標準均依各該基金公司之規定。
9. 委託人因不同時間進場，將有不同之投資績效，過去之績效亦不代表未來績效之保證。基金配息率不代表基金報酬率，且過去配息率不代表未來配息率；基金淨值可能因市場因素而上下波動，投資人於獲配息時，宜一併注意基
金淨值之變動。又投資新興市場可能比投資已開發國家有較大的價格波動及流動性較低的風險，委託人應慎選投資標的。
10. 部分基金可能持有衍生性商品部位，該部位可能超過基金淨資產價值之100%，並可能因而造成基金淨值高度波動及衍生其他風險。

11. 基金的配息可能由基金的收益或本金中支付。任何涉及由本金支出的部分，可能導致原始投資金額減損；且部分基金配息前未先扣除應負擔之費用。由本金支付配息之相關資料，投資人可至證券投資信託事業或總代理人之公司
網站查詢。
12. 非投資等級債券基金（本類基金主要係投資於非投資等級之高風險債券且配息來源可能為本金）（主要特色係投資前揭基金之組合基金亦同），適合能承受較高風險之『非保守型』委託人且不宜占投資組合過高的比重。由於非
投資等級債券之信用評等未達投資等級或未經信用評等，且對利率變動的敏感度甚高，可能會因利率上升、市場流動性下降，或債券發行機構違約不支付本金、利息或破產而蒙受虧損，不適合無法承擔相關風險之委託人；又如
係本國投信機構募集者，則可能投資於符合美國Rule 144A規定具有私募性質之債券，雖其投資總金額不得超過基金淨資產價值之百分之三十，然該債券具有債券發行人違約之信用風險、利率風險、流動性風險、交易對手風險
及因財務訊息揭露不完整或因價格不透明導致波動性較大之風險，投資人應審慎評估；另證券投資信託事業募集之債券型基金、平衡型基金、不動產證券化基金、以追蹤、模擬或複製標的指數表現之指數股票型基金（ETF）及

指數型基金或募集之基金投資新興市場國家債券達基金淨資產價值之百分之六十以上者，或有可能投資符合美國Rule 144A規定具有私募性質之債券，亦應留意相關風險。
13. 投資於中國之基金應遵守中華民國相關法令之規定：境外基金 (不含經金管會核准向證交所申請上市或交易之境外 ETF)如有投資大陸地區之有價證券者，以掛牌上市有價證券及銀行間債券市場為限 ，且投資前述有價證券總金額
不得超過該境外基金淨資產價值之百分之二十(境外基金機構依鼓勵境外基金深耕計畫向金管會申請並經認可者得適用鼓勵境外基金深耕計畫所列放寬前揭投資總金額上限至百分之四十之優惠措施 )；證券投資信託基金雖可投資
大陸地區之有價證券，惟仍應符合信託契約、公開說明書、證券投資信託基金管理辦法等相關規定及受到基金公司QFII額度之限制。又投資人須留意中國市場之政府政策、法令、會計稅務制度、經濟與市場等變動所可能產生之
投資風險。
14. 各基金於公開（含簡式）說明書或投資人須知揭露之風險報酬等級，係依據投信投顧公會「基金風險報酬等級分類標準」而訂定，本公司亦依據投信投顧公會「基金風險報酬等級分類標準」訂定基金產品風險等級。

富管理客服專線或透過財富管理客服信箱服務，查詢申訴案件處理進度。

Editorial 
Opportunities from the bond sell-off  
Strategy summary: The global bond sell-off is creating tactical 
and medium-term opportunities. In the US, September’s yield 
surge has improved the risk -reward in 10 -year government 
bonds, presenting an attractive entry point for investors with a 
6-12-month horizon. We believe bond and money markets are 
overly hawkish about the Fed outlook, given disinflation is likely 
to return as impact of oil prices and tariffs ease next year. 
In equities, we stick to quality, especially sectors riding the AI -
led earnings boom. The upcoming Q3 earnings season is likely 
to show AI -investments are powering sectors beyond the 
technology and communications services, such as financials, 
materials and power and electrification. We remain constructive 
on the semiconductor and memory segments. We also expect 
the US Dollar to peak in the next few weeks along with short -
to-medium-term US bond yields. This creates opportunities for 
AUD corporate bonds, Emerging Market USD bonds and gold. 
Attractive risk -reward in US 10 -year government bonds.  
The US 10-year bond yield rose more than 50bps in September 
(biggest monthly rise since 2022) and 115bps this year, amid 
expectations of economic resilience, higher Fed rates, fuel -
driven inflation, AI-related capital demand and fiscal concerns. 
Valuations appear close to fair value, improving the 6-12-month 
risk-reward. Bond arithmetic suggests losses of less than 1% if 
the yield rises to 6%, vs. more than 10% gains if it falls to 4.5%. 
Resilient US economic activity, but softer -than-expected 
inflation. September data showed robust US employment 
(jobless claims, ADP) and core capital-goods orders. However, 
consumer confidence remains depressed, real disposable 
income is flat and households are drawing on savings to sustain 
spending. Against this divided backdrop, core PCE inflation - 
the Fed’s preferred gauge - was softer than expected at 0.2% 
m/m and 3.0% y/y. A revised method to measure PCE inflation 
also indicate that annual core PCE inflation was 35bps lower 
than previously reported, narrowing the gap with CPI inflation. 
Markets appear overly hawkish on the Fed. Money markets 
price about 85bps of tightening over the next 12 months. A 
strong labour market (September’s non -farm payrolls data is 
due tonight) may justify one or two further hikes, but aggressive 
tightening after the sharp rise in bond yields – which has 
already tightened financial conditions - could threaten financial 
stability. US small- and mid-cap equities have already fallen 8–
9% from recent peaks over the past couple of months, 
materially underperforming large caps, as energy and 
borrowing-cost concerns intensify. W hile some Fed speakers 
remain hawkish, we  agree with New York Fed President 
Williams’ that there is no urgency to tighten. We see little 
chance of an October Fed rate hike and expect inflation data 
for Sep-Nov determining whether the Fed moves in December. 
Stay with quality equities and AI beneficiaries.  US large 
caps have absorbed the rise in bond yields relatively well, 
supported by expectations of robust earnings growth led by AI-
related investment. We continue to favour quality companies 
and sectors benefiting from this AI cycle. Semiconductors and 
memory remain preferred exposures: structural AI demand 
should extend the memory upcycle, while long -term supply 
agreements provide better revenue visibility. The opportunity is 
also broadening beyond technology and communication 
services into financials, materials, power and electrification. 
Opportunities in AUD corporate bonds and gold.  The USD 
rebound alongside higher US yields offers a tactical opportunity 
to add higher -yielding Emerging Market USD and non -USD 
bonds. We remain positive on AUD corporate bonds, which 
offer a yield premium over comparable US credit. Our 
constructive medium-term AUD/USD view could add returns for 
USD-based investors over the next 6 –12 months. We would 
also use gold’s recent pullback to add exposure, supported by 
sustained Emerging Market central bank demand. We continue 
to view USD 4,000/oz as important technical support for gold.  
—  Rajat Bhattacharya

Top client questions 
  Do you expect more RBA rate hikes after its less hawkish 
guidance? What is your view on the AUD and AUD bonds? 
Our view: We expect the RBA to leave rates unchanged, but 
retain the option to hike again should inflation intensify. We 
maintain our medium-term constructive AUD view and maintain 
our opportunistically bullish AUD corporate bonds idea. 
Rationale: The RBA’s forward guidance remains data dependent, 
explicitly allowing further tightening if needed as inflation remains too 
high.  At 4.60%, Australia’s cash rate is currently 60–85bp above the 
Fed’s 3.75–4.00% range. Thus, even with our two additional 25bp s 
Fed hikes expectation, Australian rates would hold a modest positive 
rate differential. We expect the AUD to strengthen once the Fed 
signals that US rates are near their peak, which should allow resilient 
Australian fundamentals to reassert themselves. Key risks are 
persistently high US yields, weaker China growth or a sharper 
deterioration in global risk sentiment. 
We believe risk-reward has become more attractive for AUD bonds 
following the bear -flattening of the AUD yield curve. Markets are 
already pricing in one more RBA hike through end-2027, presenting 
an attractive opportunity to earn a yield even as increasingly 
restrictive financial conditions reinforc e disinflation over time. 
Meanwhile, the positive interest rate differential with the US supports 
the currency outlook, supporting the case for AUD bonds. 
—  Vincent Tan, Senior Investment Strategist 
— Cedric Lam, Senior Investment Strategist 
— Iris Yuen, Investment Strategist 
  What does the incoming US data tell us about the path for 
US 10-year bond yields from here? 
Our view: The risk -reward in US 10 -year yields has turned 
attractive following their move higher amid incoming US data. 
Rationale: Recent US data has indeed started to show softer-than-
expected inflation. However, market technicals are likely temporarily 
delaying a move lower in bond yields, with reports suggesting the  
rise in yields is creating forced selling amongst hedge funds and real 
estate investment trusts. Nevertheless, we do not expect this to be 
an extended selloff. We have initiated an opportunistic bullish idea 
on US 10-year government bonds this week and continue to believe 
this idea offers an attractive risk/reward on a 6-12-month horizon as 
yields become attractive enough to entice buyer flow to return. On a 
broader allocation basis, we retain our structural preference for 3-7-
year maturities in bonds. 
— Cedric Lam, Senior Investment Strategist

Standard Chartered Bank

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  How should investors position in the GBP ahead of the UK 
budget announcement next month? 
Our view: We maintain a medium-term constructive GBP view, 
but keep near-term exposure measured ahead of the 28 October 
Budget. We would view any pre -Budget weakness as an 
opportunity to gradually build GBP exposure. 
Rationale: The government’s decision to adjust the pension triple 
lock from 2030 is an early indication of greater willingness to tackle 
longer-term spending pressures, reinforcing Chancellor Healey’s 
commitment to fiscal discipline and meeting the fiscal rules. Markets 
are likely to scrutinise the Budget closely for evidence that this 
discipline extends to near -term tax and spending decisions. A 
credible Budget should help contain pressure on UK government 
bond yields and support GBP. Any fiscal slippage remains a key risk.  
Near term, we prefer measured GBP exposure as elevated US yields 
and repricing towards further Fed tightening could continue to 
support the USD. Over the medium term, we expect GBP to rebound 
as the Fed eventually signals the peak of its tightening cycle. 
—  Vincent Tan, Senior Investment Strategist 
—  Iris Yuen, Investment Strategist 
  How would different US mid-term election outcome 
scenarios affect the outlook for US equities? 
Our view: We believe election outcomes will have a limited 
impact over the next 12 months. We remain positive on US 
equities driven by fundamental earnings growth.  
Rationale: Over the past 50 years, the ruling US President’s party 
has retained control of both chambers of Congress on only two 
occasions (1978 and 2002). All other elections result ed in an 
opposition sweep or a divided government.  
Based on the latest prediction markets, it appears that an opposition 
sweep (both the House and Senate flips from Republican to 
Democrats) is more likely than a divided government ( in which the 
House flips to Democrats , while the Senate remains under 
Republican control). A scenario where Republicans retain a majority 
is least likely per prediction markets. 
While mid -term elections may introduce near -term volatility, we 
caution against overreaction. Historically, the average return for the 
S&P 500 following an opposition sweep is 17% over the next 12 
months, but the average return following a divided government is not 
significantly worse, at 15%. We prefer to base our investment views 
on fundamental earnings growth, where investor attention should 
focus on the upcoming Q3 earnings season. 
—  Jason Wong, Senior Equity Analyst

### Page 6

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 2 October 2026

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  What is the outlook for  the memory chip and broader 
semiconductors industry? 
Our view: We remain positive on semiconductors and memory 
chips. Structural AI demand should prolong the memory 
upcycle while long-term agreements improve revenue visibility. 
Rationale: Recent industry commentary points to tighter memory 
supply-demand conditions through 2028, reinforcing our view that AI 
demand is likely to extend the memory upcycle. Furthermore, adoption 
of long-term agreements improves revenue and earnings visibility. 
Despite debates around “Pacing the Frontier”, we have seen a flurry 
of new model releases, along with the introduction of new consumer 
AI agents capable of comparing prices online and making 
purchases. These are early days, but the path is open for a business-
to-agent (B2A) economy where businesses increasingly interact with 
AI assistants acting on customers’ behalf. As these applications are 
increasingly inference intensive, wider adoption should support 
demand for memory, with its share of global semiconductor 
revenues projected to rise from 30.7% in 2025 to 66.7% in 2027. 
Against this backdrop, we maintain our Opportunistic idea on Global 
Semiconductors. Given memory  chips’ cyclical nature, investors 
should avoid overconcentration in memory names and maintain a 
diversified exposure across Big Tech and semiconductors. 
—  Ryan Goh, Investment Strategist 
  Does a US bond yield stress test raise the risk to your 
constructive view on US equities? 
Our view: We have a target for the S&P 500 index of 8400 over 
the next 12 months. A scenario of a sustained rise in the US 10-
year government bond yield to 5.6% could lead to negative 
returns from the current S&P500 level (7,666). 
Rationale: Our base case 12-month target for the S&P 500 is 8,400, 
which assumes 2027 S&P 500 EPS of USD 420 and a 20x forward 
P/E multiple. A rise in bond yields would have an impact on equity 
valuations, as a higher discount rate reduces the present value of 
future earnings. W e stress -test our target under different 10 -year 
yield and earnings scenarios.  
A rise in bond yields would arguably be associated with a stronger 
growth environment and hence, even higher earnings. However, 
assuming unchanged EPS at our base case of USD 420, a 
(sustained) rise in the 10 -year bond yield to 5.6% would lead to 
negative returns from the current level  if not offset by a further 
improvement in earnings growth . However, in o ur base case  we 
expect the 10-year yield is more likely to fall from here. 
—  Fook Hien Yap, Senior Investment Strategist

### Page 2

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 16 October 2026

Charts of the week: No capitulation

A hotter-than-expected US inflation report triggered a pullback in risk assets, but there are few signs of capitulation so far 
US core and supercore inflation; US inflation breadth*    Our ‘Fear and Greed’ indicator

Source: Bloomberg, Standard Chartered; *share of US consumer inflation components rising more than 3%

Editorial 
Staying defensive and diversified  
Strategy summary:  Two weeks ago, we turned defensive 
towards equities, locking in semiconductor equity gains amid 
elevated bond volatility, narrowing market breadth and political 
uncertainty before the US mid -term elections. The near -term 
outlook reflected a less favourable risk-reward balance. Since 
we turned defensive , the Nasdaq 100 index has recorded its 
biggest weekly decline in three months as September’s hotter-
than-expected US inflation report briefly lifted bond yields. 
We have extended our defensive stance this week, 
downgrading US Communication Services equities to Neutral 
and upgrading US Healthcare to Overweight. We also closed 
our opportunistic bullish Communication Services idea and 
initiated a bullish idea on Healthcare, where earnings visibility 
and defensive characteristics appear more attractive. In fixed 
income, we see opportunities in US 10-year government bonds. 
We await clearer AI profit margins and spending guidance, 
broader earnings growth or lower bond and equity market 
volatility before turning positive on equities. Until then, 
diversification and disciplined entry levels remain important. 
Hot US inflation triggers equity pullback; not hot enough 
for an October Fed hike:  Risk assets pulled back after 
September’s hotter-than-expected inflation data (headline 3.7% 
y/y; core 2.6% y/y). Higher gasoline and diesel prices were the 
main drivers, but passthrough appeared in airline fares, used 
cars and transportation services, showing pressures were not 
confined to energy. Almost half of inflation components are still 
rising above 3%, a measure recently cited by Fed Chair Warsh. 
Producer inflation also beat estimates. The reports revive the 
possibility of an October Fed rate hike, but we expect a divided 
Fed to weigh it against September’s soft jobs report and 
narrowly hold this month. A December hike appears more likely 
if inflation remains firm . Although Fed Chair Warsh did not 
comment on policy at an IMF speech this week, New York Fed 
chief Williams said he saw no need to hike in 2027. Our base 
case remains two further hikes by June 2027. 
Limited equity pullback so far.  Equities fell sharply after the 
US inflation report, but the pullback is limited. The Nasdaq 100 
is 4.5% below its record high, while the S&P 500 remains close 
to all -time highs despite the latest decline. The S&P 500 
volatility index (VIX) has risen from below 15 to 1 8.2, still well 
under the 25 –30 range typically associated with capitulation 
and forced selling. Meanwhile, US government bond volatility 
(MOVE) remains elevated after surging since September.  Our 
stock-bond quant model turned more negativ e (-2) this week, 
signalling elevated equity volatility in the near term.  
When to turn more constructive on stocks?  We would turn 
more constructive when capitulation signals align: the Nasdaq 
100 approaches a 10% drawdown, the VIX rises to 25 –30 and 
our ‘Fear and Greed’ index  enters “extreme fear”. Greater 
confidence would also require easing concerns about AI 
spending and technology firms’ exposure to neocloud revenue, 
lower bond volatility and our stock-bond model turning positive 
again. Until these conditions emerge, volatility may remain 
elevated, favouring a defensive equity stance. Political risk 
should ease after the US mid -term elections. We continue to 
seek selective opportunities following excessive corrections. 
US financials remain a preferred sector after Q3 earnings:  
US large banks’ earnings were mixed, but underlying trends 
remain encouraging. Healthy loan and deposit growth supports 
net interest income, while resilient consumer spending, wealth 
management activity and a solid M&A and refinancing pipeline 
strengthen the outlook. Credit risks appear manageable . We 
remain constructive and favour stronger, diversified franchises. 
US government bonds offer value as yields spike:  The 
reversal in US government bond yields after their initial inflation-
driven spike suggests limited further upside. The 10 -year yield 
briefly reached 5.32% before slumping as falling equities 
prompted short-covering. We expect a near -term 5.00–5.25% 
range and view moves above 5.25% as attractive entry points 
for our opportunistic 10-year government bond idea. Structural 
pressures, including term premium and increasing supply to 
fund fiscal deficits and AI investments, prevent us from turning 
outright bullish on longer maturities. We, therefore, continue to 
prefer 3–7-year maturities in broader bond allocations.  
—  Rajat Bhattacharya and Sundeep Gantori

### Page 4

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 16 October 2026

Top client questions 
  How does the September 2026 US Consumer Price Index 
(CPI) report alter your view on the USD and bond yields?   
Our view: The hotter-than-expected CPI print raises the odds of 
an October Fed hike, though we still expect a hold followed by 
a rate hike in December. In the near term, we maintain our view 
that the US 10 -year government bond yield will range between 
5.00% and 5.25%, while  the USD will remain supported as 
markets reassess the likelihood of further Fed hikes. Additional 
gains would depend partly on markets pricing a faster or more 
prolonged tightening cycle, while clearer signs of an 
approaching policy rate peak could see the USD ease back. 
Rationale: September US consumer inflation  came in above 
consensus across the board – headline CPI rose 3.7% y/y (vs. 3.6% 
estimate), and core CPI rose 2.6% y/y (vs. 2.5% estimate) – marking 
the fastest headline acceleration in five months. This shifts the 
balance of risks modestly hawkish . Unsurprisingly, the market -
implied probability of an October Fed hike rose over 50% overnight 
but closed the day at 32.7% – 9ppt higher than Tuesday’s close. 
We believe a split Fed will weigh this inflation report against the soft 
September jobs report and narrowly vote to keep rates on hold this 
month. Our base case remains for two more Fed hikes  by June 
2027. A December rate hike  now looks more like a done deal, 
especially as persistently higher oil prices keep headline and core 
inflation elevated for at least the next few months. Any re-escalation 
of the Middle East conflict after the  US mid-term elections would 
exert pressure on the Fed, although we believe Republicans would  
show greater restraint in US foreign action if they suffer steep Senate 
losses, which looks increasingly likely. 
The intra-day price action in US government bonds was instructive. 
The US 10-year yield briefly spiked to 5.32% on the print before 
closing materially lower, as pre -existing bearish positioning was 
unwound and Fed speakers (Barkin, Goolsbee) stopped well short 
of signalling an imminent move. This dynamic reinforces our view 
that the technical selling pressure driving yields towards the upper 
end of our range is fading. Structural drivers – term premium, fiscal 
supply concerns and ‘higher for longer’ Fed repricing – remain intact 
and keep us from turning outright bullish on duration. However, our 
opportunistic bullish idea in US 10-year government bonds initiated 
at September-end remains, as we continue to view spikes in US 10-
year yields over 5.25% as a good risk-reward entry. From a longer-
term portfolio standpoint, we continue to favour the 3-7-year tenor. 
On FX, elevated US government bond yields should sustain the 
USD’s interest rate appeal. However, expected hikes alone will not 
drive further appreciation. Stronger data or firmer Fed guidance 
would provide fresh support, while evidence that tightening is 
nearing completion would temper the USD’s strength. 
—  Vincent Tan, Senior Investment Strategist 
— Ray Heung, Senior Investment Strategist

The US Dollar Index (DXY) is likely to stay 
rangebound between 102 and 103, supported by 
elevated US government bond yields
